Well, no, not exactly right after the divorce itself. The divorce was hard, of course, but I actually thought I was handling it pretty well for a while. I was really trying to stay positive.

There was just this whole financial mess that came up from the settlement, and... I don't know, it just felt like too much all at once. Well, um... yes, it really has. I hate to admit it because I've always prided myself on being reliable, but... things have gotten a bit messy lately.

It actually got bad enough that I... well, I ended up being sent to the emergency department recently because I just... I didn't feel safe with my own thoughts anymore. 

I'm so sorry, I know that sounds really intense and I probably should have handled it better before it got to that point. I really want to be doing better than this. Does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm not oversharing...

Well... I guess I'd say I'm still trying to keep up with things. I mean, I don't want to just sit around and do nothing, you know? But... I suppose things I usually like doing haven't felt quite as fun lately.

Um... well, my appetite has been pretty normal, I think. I mean, I've been trying really hard to make sure I still eat three meals a day, even when I'm feeling a bit down. I know how important it is to keep my strength up.

Um... sleep has been... well, I've really been trying to get my eight hours, because I know how important a good routine is. I've been setting a bedtime and trying to wind down, but... I guess I have been having a little bit of trouble lately. 

It's probably just normal stress, right? But sometimes I just... I lie awake staring at the ceiling, and my brain just starts replaying all these little things—like bills I need to pay, or silly mistakes I made at work. I try really hard to push it away. And then, other times, I'll wake up way before dawn, and I just feel like I never actually went to sleep at all. 

I'm sure it's just because I have a lot on my plate right now, and I'm trying to handle it all as best as I can. It's pretty normal to lose a little sleep when you're overwhelmed, isn't it? I just... I hope I'm not doing a bad job of managing everything.

Um... well, I wouldn't say "unusually" tired, exactly. I mean, I'm still showing up to work and getting things done. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining or just being lazy.

You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

Um... well, I really try to stay on top of everything at work, I promise. I've always prided myself on being very sharp and reliable. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, I have been struggling a little bit with focus lately.

I just can’t shake this heavy, hopeless feeling, no matter how hard I try to push through it. It really just feels like everything I built after the divorce has collapsed, and I’m just sitting under it. \n\nI hope it's okay that I made an appointment for this. I know I should be stronger, and I really just want to get back to normal so I'm not wasting your time. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things day to day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, it really has. I hate to admit it because I've always prided myself on being reliable, but... things have gotten a bit messy lately. \n\nAt work, I started... well, I guess I was making some questionable calls on project designs. I actually ended up kind of falling apart in a few meetings, which was so embarrassing. My supervisor actually had to step in and take me off active project decisions for now. I feel terrible about it, I really hope they don't think less of me. I just couldn't focus.\n\nAnd at home... I just kind of stopped keeping up with everything. Basic routines, cooking, cleaning... it all just felt like too much. It actually got bad enough that I... well, I ended up being sent to the emergency department recently because I just... I didn't feel safe with my own thoughts anymore. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds really intense and I probably should have handled it better before it got to that point. I really want to be doing better than this. Does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm not oversharing...\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess... I mean, I wouldn't say I've had a plan or anything like that. I know how terrible that would be for everyone around me, and I really wouldn't want to cause anyone that kind of pain. \n\nBut... I have been having these thoughts lately where I just... I kind of wish I wouldn't wake up. Like it would just be easier if everything stopped. It got a little bit intense the other day, and it actually scared me quite a bit. That's... that's why I ended up calling the employee assistance program at work before I went to the ED. I just wanted to make sure I was doing the right thing and getting help before I did something stupid. \n\nI feel awful even saying it out loud. I know I shouldn't be thinking that way. I'm really trying to get better, I promise. Is it normal to feel like that when you're this stressed out? I hope you don't think I'm crazy or anything...\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess I'd say I'm still trying to keep up with things. I hope that doesn't sound too concerning...\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Um... sleep has been... well, I've really been trying to get my eight hours, because I know how important a good routine is. I feel a little silly even admitting it, but... yes, it does feel a lot stronger than it probably should. \n\nHonestly, that's... that's the main reason I came in to see you today. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things day to day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, it really has. I actually ended up kind of falling apart in a few meetings, which was so embarrassing. My supervisor actually had to step in and take me off active project decisions for now. I didn't feel safe with my own thoughts anymore. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds really intense and I probably should have handled it better before it got to that point. I kind of wish I wouldn't wake up. Like it would just be easier if everything stopped. It got a little bit intense the other day, and it actually scared me quite a bit. I guess it started with the financial stuff, but... it's kind of become more than that now. I really hope I'm not sounding like I'm just feeling sorry for myself, but... it's just this constant, heavy feeling that I can't seem to shake. \n\nIt's like... even when I try to focus on other things, or when I'm at work—I'm a civil engineer, so I really need to be able to focus—it's just always there. That hopeless feeling. Like I mentioned before, it really just feels like everything I tried to rebuild is just gone, and I'm just stuck under the weight of it all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. I know I should be handling this better.\n\nDoctor: Before this past month, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I really don't think so. I mean, I've had normal bad days, you know? Like everyone does. But nothing that lasted for weeks or felt this... heavy. I’ve always been pretty good at just pushing through things and staying productive. \n\nI hope I'm not forgetting anything important... I really want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information. But as far as I can remember, this is the first time it's ever been like this for me. Is that okay? I'm sorry if that's not very helpful for figuring things out, I just want to be as accurate as I can for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this sadness is stronger than you would normally expect from the financial settlement problem?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I think so. I mean, I keep telling myself it's just money, right? People deal with financial stress every day, so I really shouldn't be letting it get to me this badly. I feel a little silly even admitting it, but... yes, it does feel a lot stronger than it probably should. \n\nHonestly, that's... that's the main reason I came in to see you today. I just can’t shake this heavy, hopeless feeling, no matter how hard I try to push through it. It really just feels like everything I built after the divorce has collapsed, and I’m just sitting under it. \n\nI hope it's okay that I made an appointment for this. I know I should be stronger, and I really just want to get back to normal so I'm not wasting your time. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things day to day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, it really has. I hate to admit it because I've always prided myself on being reliable, but... things have gotten a bit messy lately. \n\nAt work, I started... well, I guess I was making some questionable calls on project designs. I actually ended up kind of falling apart in a few meetings, which was so embarrassing. My supervisor actually had to step in and take me off active project decisions for now. I feel terrible about it, I really hope they don't think less of me. I just couldn't focus.\n\nAnd at home... I just kind of stopped keeping up with everything. Basic routines, cooking, cleaning... it all just felt like too much. It actually got bad enough that I... well, I ended up being sent to the emergency department recently because I just... I didn't feel safe with my own thoughts anymore. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds really intense and I probably should have handled it better before it got to that point. I really want to be doing better than this. Does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm not oversharing...\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess... I mean, I wouldn't say I've had a plan or anything like that. I know how terrible that would be for everyone around me, and I really wouldn't want to cause anyone that kind of pain. \n\nBut... I have been having these thoughts lately where I just... I kind of wish I wouldn't wake up. Like it would just be easier if everything stopped. It got a little bit intense the other day, and it actually scared me quite a bit. That's... that's why I ended up calling the employee assistance program at work before I went to the ED. I just wanted to make sure I was doing the right thing and getting help before I did something stupid. \n\nI feel awful even saying it out loud. I know I shouldn't be thinking that way. I'm really trying to get better, I promise. Is it normal to feel like that when you're this stressed out? I hope you don't think I'm crazy or anything...\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess I'd say I'm still trying to keep up with things. I mean, I don't want to just sit around and do nothing, you know? But... I suppose things I usually like doing haven't felt quite as fun lately. \n\nLike, I usually love going hiking on the weekends, or even just working on little DIY projects around the house. I've tried to force myself to do them because I know I should, but... I just haven't really felt that spark, I guess. I kind of just end up staring at the wall or going back to bed. \n\nI'm sure it's just because I'm so tired from everything else going on. I really am trying to stay active and positive, I promise. Do you think that's just part of being this stressed out? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is...\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or feeling certain of things that other people say aren’t true?\n\nPatient: Oh! Um... no. No, definitely not. I mean, I know I've been really overwhelmed lately, and I'm so sorry if I made it sound like things were getting completely out of hand. But I haven't experienced anything like that. No voices or... or believing things that aren't real. \n\nI'm still very grounded in reality, I promise. I'm just having a hard time managing my stress right now. That's... that's normal for someone going through a rough patch, right? I really hope you don't think I'm completely losing my mind...\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a big change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Um... well, my appetite has been pretty normal, I think. I mean, I've been trying really hard to make sure I still eat three meals a day, even when I'm feeling a bit down. I know how important it is to keep my strength up. \n\nMaybe... maybe I've been eating just a tiny bit less than usual? Just because I've been so tired, you know? Sometimes fixing a whole meal just feels like a lot of work. But I'm definitely not starving myself or anything like that. I've been making sure to at least have some toast or a piece of fruit. \n\nI'm doing my best to take care of myself, I really am. It's just hard when you're feeling so drained. That's a pretty common reaction to stress, isn't it? Just not feeling super hungry all the time? I hope that doesn't sound too concerning...\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Um... sleep has been... well, I've really been trying to get my eight hours, because I know how important a good routine is. I've been setting a bedtime and trying to wind down, but... I guess I have been having a little bit of trouble lately. \n\nIt's probably just normal stress, right? But sometimes I just... I lie awake staring at the ceiling, and my brain just starts replaying all these little things—like bills I need to pay, or silly mistakes I made at work. I try really hard to push it away. And then, other times, I'll wake up way before dawn, and I just feel like I never actually went to sleep at all. \n\nI'm sure it's just because I have a lot on my plate right now, and I'm trying to handle it all as best as I can. It's pretty normal to lose a little sleep when you're overwhelmed, isn't it? I just... I hope I'm not doing a bad job of managing everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say \"unusually\" tired, exactly. I mean, I'm still showing up to work and getting things done. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining or just being lazy. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, things have felt a little bit heavier lately. Like, getting through a normal workday kind of feels like... like I'm dragging wet concrete? And sometimes just doing little things, like taking a shower or opening my laptop to start a project, it just takes more energy than I seem to have right now. \n\nI'm still pushing through it, though! I know everyone gets tired, especially with a demanding job. It's totally normal to feel a bit drained when you're working hard, right? I just hope I'm not letting anyone down by being a little slower than usual...\n\nDoctor: For the past month, have you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I've given up or anything. I'm really trying to keep a positive attitude. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, and I don't want to sound ungrateful or dramatic. \n\nBut... I guess the reason I finally made the appointment is because... well, I just can't seem to shake this really heavy feeling. It's just... it's been hard to feel hopeful lately. You know, after my divorce, I worked so hard to rebuild my life and get everything back on track. And I did a good job, I really did. But lately, it just kind of feels like... like everything I built has collapsed, and I'm just sort of sitting underneath all the rubble. \n\nI'm still trying to stay strong, though! I know everyone goes through rough patches, and it's normal to feel a little down sometimes, right? I just... I hope it doesn't make me sound weak for struggling with it this much.\n\nDoctor: During the past month, have you felt down or hopeless nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm sorry, I know I sort of just rambled about this a second ago. I hope I'm answering your questions right. But... yes. To be completely honest, it really has been almost every single day this past month. \n\nLike I was saying, I just... I can't seem to shake this heavy, hopeless feeling. It really does feel like everything I tried so hard to build after the divorce has just completely collapsed, and I'm just trapped sitting under it all. \n\nI really hate admitting that out loud, because it makes me sound so pessimistic, and I promise I'm usually a very capable person at work and everywhere else. I'm really trying my best to push through it. It's okay to admit when things feel a little too heavy, isn't it? I just hope I'm not coming across as too negative...\n\nDoctor: Do you still have moments when you feel okay, as long as you're not thinking about the financial settlement?\n\nPatient: Um... I wish I could say yes, I really do. I don't want to sound like I'm just wallowing in it, because I promise I'm trying my best to stay positive and keep moving forward.
